[?2004h]0;student@lab000001: ~[01;32mstudent@lab000001[00m:[01;34m~[00m$ man chmod
[?2004l[?1049h[22;0;0t[?1h=CHMOD(1)                                                      User Commands                                                      CHMOD(1)[m
[m
[1mNAME[0m[m
       chmod - change file mode bits[m
[m
[1mSYNOPSIS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mMODE[24m[[4m,MODE[24m]... [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mOCTAL-MODE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4m--reference=RFILE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
[m
[1mDESCRIPTION[0m[m
       This manual page documents the GNU version of [1mchmod[0m.  [1mchmod[0m changes the file mode bits of each given file according to [4mmode[24m, which[m
       can be either a symbolic representation of changes to make, or an octal number representing the bit pattern for the new mode bits.[m
[m
       The format of a symbolic mode is [[1mugoa[0m...][[[1m-+=[0m][[4mperms[24m...]...], where [4mperms[24m is either zero or more letters from the set [1mrwxXst[0m, or[m
       a single letter from the set [1mugo[0m.  Multiple symbolic modes can be given, separated by commas.[m
[m
       A  combination of the letters [1mugoa[0m controls which users' access to the file will be changed: the user who owns it ([1mu[0m), other users[m
       in the file's group ([1mg[0m), other users not in the file's group ([1mo[0m), or all users ([1ma[0m).  If none of these are given, the effect is  as[m
       if ([1ma[0m) were given, but bits that are set in the umask are not affected.[m
[m
       The operator [1m+[0m causes the selected file mode bits to be added to the existing file mode bits of each file; [1m-[0m causes them to be re‐[m
       moved; and [1m=[0m causes them to be added and causes unmentioned bits to be removed except that a directory's unmentioned set user  and[m
       group ID bits are not affected.[m
[m
       The letters [1mrwxXst[0m select file mode bits for the affected users: read ([1mr[0m), write ([1mw[0m), execute (or search for directories) ([1mx[0m), ex‐[m
       ecute/search only if the file is a directory or already has execute permission for some user ([1mX[0m), set user or group ID  on  execu‐[m
       tion ([1ms[0m), restricted deletion flag or sticky bit ([1mt[0m).  Instead of one or more of these letters, you can specify exactly one of the[m
       letters [1mugo[0m: the permissions granted to the user who owns the file ([1mu[0m), the permissions granted to other users who are members  of[m
       the file's group ([1mg[0m), and the permissions granted to users that are in neither of the two preceding categories ([1mo[0m).[m
[m
       A  numeric mode is from one to four octal digits (0-7), derived by adding up the bits with values 4, 2, and 1.  Omitted digits are[m
       assumed to be leading zeros.  The first digit selects the set user ID (4) and set group ID (2) and restricted deletion  or  sticky[m
       (1)  attributes.   The  second digit selects permissions for the user who owns the file: read (4), write (2), and execute (1); the[m
       third selects permissions for other users in the file's group, with the same values; and the fourth for other  users  not  in  the[m
       file's group, with the same values.[m
[m
       [1mchmod[0m never changes the permissions of symbolic links; the [1mchmod[0m system call cannot change their permissions.  This is not a prob‐[m
       lem since the permissions of symbolic links are never used.  However, for each symbolic link listed on  the  command  line,  [1mchmod[0m[m
       changes  the permissions of the pointed-to file.  In contrast, [1mchmod[0m ignores symbolic links encountered during recursive directory[m
       traversals.[m
[m
[1mSETUID[0m [1mAND[0m [1mSETGID[0m [1mBITS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m clears the set-group-ID bit of a regular file if the file's group ID does not match the user's effective group ID or one  of[m
       the user's supplementary group IDs, unless the user has appropriate privileges.  Additional restrictions may cause the set-user-ID[m
       and set-group-ID bits of [4mMODE[24m or [4mRFILE[24m to be ignored.  This behavior depends on the policy and  functionality  of  the  underlying[m
       [1mchmod[0m system call.  When in doubt, check the underlying system behavior.[m
[m
       For  directories  [1mchmod[0m preserves set-user-ID and set-group-ID bits unless you explicitly specify otherwise.  You can set or clear[m
       the bits with symbolic modes like [1mu+s[0m and [1mg-s[0m.  To clear these bits for directories with a numeric  mode  requires  an  additional[m
       leading zero, or leading = like [1m00755[0m , or [1m=755[0m[m
[m
[1mRESTRICTED[0m [1mDELETION[0m [1mFLAG[0m [1mOR[0m [1mSTICKY[0m [1mBIT[0m[m
       The  restricted  deletion  flag or sticky bit is a single bit, whose interpretation depends on the file type.  For directories, it[m
       prevents unprivileged users from removing or renaming a file in the directory unless they own the file or the directory;  this  is[m
       called the [4mrestricted[24m [4mdeletion[24m [4mflag[24m for the directory, and is commonly found on world-writable directories like [1m/tmp[0m.  For regular[m
       files on some older systems, the bit saves the program's text image on the swap device so it will load more quickly when run; this[m
       is called the [4msticky[24m [4mbit[24m.[m
[m
[1mOPTIONS[0m[m
       Change the mode of each FILE to MODE.  With [1m--reference[0m, change the mode of each FILE to that of RFILE.[m
[m
       [1m-c[0m, [1m--changes[0m[m
              like verbose but report only when a change is made[m
[m
       [1m-f[0m, [1m--silent[0m, [1m--quiet[0m[m
              suppress most error messages[m
[m
       [1m-v[0m, [1m--verbose[0m[m
              output a diagnostic for every file processed[m
[m
       [1m--no-preserve-root[0m[m
              do not treat '/' specially (the default)[m
[m
       [1m--preserve-root[0m[m
              fail to operate recursively on '/'[m
[m
       [1m--reference[0m=[4mRFILE[24m[m
              use RFILE's mode instead of MODE values[m
[m
       [1m-R[0m, [1m--recursive[0m[m
              change files and directories recursively[m
[m
       [1m--help[0m display this help and exit[m
[m
       [1m--version[0m[m
[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 1 (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K              output version information and exit[m
[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 2 (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K[m
[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 3 (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K       Each MODE is of the form '[ugoa]*([-+=]([rwxXst]*|[ugo]))+|[-+=][0-7]+'.[m
[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 4 (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K[m
[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 5 (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KBB[K[1mAUTHOR[0m[m

[?2004l[?1049h[22;0;0t[?1h=CHMOD(1)                                                      User Commands                                                      CHMOD(1)[m
[m
[1mNAME[0m[m
       chmod - change file mode bits[m
[m
[1mSYNOPSIS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mMODE[24m[[4m,MODE[24m]... [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mOCTAL-MODE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4m--reference=RFILE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
[m
[1mDESCRIPTION[0m[m
       This manual page documents the GNU version of [1mchmod[0m.  [1mchmod[0m changes the file mode bits of each given file according to [4mmode[24m, which[m
       can be either a symbolic representation of changes to make, or an octal number representing the bit pattern for the new mode bits.[m
[m
       The format of a symbolic mode is [[1mugoa[0m...][[[1m-+=[0m][[4mperms[24m...]...], where [4mperms[24m is either zero or more letters from the set [1mrwxXst[0m, or[m
       a single letter from the set [1mugo[0m.  Multiple symbolic modes can be given, separated by commas.[m
[m
       A  combination of the letters [1mugoa[0m controls which users' access to the file will be changed: the user who owns it ([1mu[0m), other users[m
       in the file's group ([1mg[0m), other users not in the file's group ([1mo[0m), or all users ([1ma[0m).  If none of these are given, the effect is  as[m
       if ([1ma[0m) were given, but bits that are set in the umask are not affected.[m
[m
       The operator [1m+[0m causes the selected file mode bits to be added to the existing file mode bits of each file; [1m-[0m causes them to be re‐[m
       moved; and [1m=[0m causes them to be added and causes unmentioned bits to be removed except that a directory's unmentioned set user  and[m
       group ID bits are not affected.[m
[m
       The letters [1mrwxXst[0m select file mode bits for the affected users: read ([1mr[0m), write ([1mw[0m), execute (or search for directories) ([1mx[0m), ex‐[m
       ecute/search only if the file is a directory or already has execute permission for some user ([1mX[0m), set user or group ID  on  execu‐[m
       tion ([1ms[0m), restricted deletion flag or sticky bit ([1mt[0m).  Instead of one or more of these letters, you can specify exactly one of the[m
       letters [1mugo[0m: the permissions granted to the user who owns the file ([1mu[0m), the permissions granted to other users who are members  of[m
       the file's group ([1mg[0m), and the permissions granted to users that are in neither of the two preceding categories ([1mo[0m).[m
[m
       A  numeric mode is from one to four octal digits (0-7), derived by adding up the bits with values 4, 2, and 1.  Omitted digits are[m
       assumed to be leading zeros.  The first digit selects the set user ID (4) and set group ID (2) and restricted deletion  or  sticky[m
       (1)  attributes.   The  second digit selects permissions for the user who owns the file: read (4), write (2), and execute (1); the[m
       third selects permissions for other users in the file's group, with the same values; and the fourth for other  users  not  in  the[m
       file's group, with the same values.[m
[m
       [1mchmod[0m never changes the permissions of symbolic links; the [1mchmod[0m system call cannot change their permissions.  This is not a prob‐[m
       lem since the permissions of symbolic links are never used.  However, for each symbolic link listed on  the  command  line,  [1mchmod[0m[m
       changes  the permissions of the pointed-to file.  In contrast, [1mchmod[0m ignores symbolic links encountered during recursive directory[m
       traversals.[m
[m
[1mSETUID[0m [1mAND[0m [1mSETGID[0m [1mBITS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m clears the set-group-ID bit of a regular file if the file's group ID does not match the user's effective group ID or one  of[m
       the user's supplementary group IDs, unless the user has appropriate privileges.  Additional restrictions may cause the set-user-ID[m
       and set-group-ID bits of [4mMODE[24m or [4mRFILE[24m to be ignored.  This behavior depends on the policy and  functionality  of  the  underlying[m
       [1mchmod[0m system call.  When in doubt, check the underlying system behavior.[m
[m
       For  directories  [1mchmod[0m preserves set-user-ID and set-group-ID bits unless you explicitly specify otherwise.  You can set or clear[m
       the bits with symbolic modes like [1mu+s[0m and [1mg-s[0m.  To clear these bits for directories with a numeric  mode  requires  an  additional[m
       leading zero, or leading = like [1m00755[0m , or [1m=755[0m[m
[m
[1mRESTRICTED[0m [1mDELETION[0m [1mFLAG[0m [1mOR[0m [1mSTICKY[0m [1mBIT[0m[m
       The  restricted  deletion  flag or sticky bit is a single bit, whose interpretation depends on the file type.  For directories, it[m
       prevents unprivileged users from removing or renaming a file in the directory unless they own the file or the directory;  this  is[m
       called the [4mrestricted[24m [4mdeletion[24m [4mflag[24m for the directory, and is commonly found on world-writable directories like [1m/tmp[0m.  For regular[m
       files on some older systems, the bit saves the program's text image on the swap device so it will load more quickly when run; this[m
       is called the [4msticky[24m [4mbit[24m.[m
[m
[1mOPTIONS[0m[m
       Change the mode of each FILE to MODE.  With [1m--reference[0m, change the mode of each FILE to that of RFILE.[m
[m
       [1m-c[0m, [1m--changes[0m[m
              like verbose but report only when a change is made[m
[m
       [1m-f[0m, [1m--silent[0m, [1m--quiet[0m[m
              suppress most error messages[m
[m
       [1m-v[0m, [1m--verbose[0m[m
              output a diagnostic for every file processed[m
[m
       [1m--no-preserve-root[0m[m
              do not treat '/' specially (the default)[m
[m
       [1m--preserve-root[0m[m
              fail to operate recursively on '/'[m
[m
       [1m--reference[0m=[4mRFILE[24m[m
              use RFILE's mode instead of MODE values[m
[m

[KESCESC[KOO[KAA[K[K[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 1/108 86% (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KDD[H[2J[H[H[2J[HCHMOD(1)                                                      User Commands                                                      CHMOD(1)[m
[m
[1mNAME[0m[m
       chmod - change file mode bits[m
[m
[1mSYNOPSIS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mMODE[24m[[4m,MODE[24m]... [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mOCTAL-MODE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4m--reference=RFILE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
[m
[1mDESCRIPTION[0m[m
       This manual page documents the GNU version of [1mchmod[0m.  [1mchmod[0m changes the file mode bits of each given file according to [4mmode[24m, which[m
       can be either a symbolic representation of changes to make, or an octal number representing the bit pattern for the new mode bits.[m
[m
       The format of a symbolic mode is [[1mugoa[0m...][[[1m-+=[0m][[4mperms[24m...]...], where [4mperms[24m is either zero or more letters from the set [1mrwxXst[0m, or[m
       a single letter from the set [1mugo[0m.  Multiple symbolic modes can be given, separated by commas.[m
[m
       A  combination of the letters [1mugoa[0m controls which users' access to the file will be changed: the user who owns it ([1mu[0m), other users[m
       in the file's group ([1mg[0m), other users not in the file's group ([1mo[0m), or all users ([1ma[0m).  If none of these are given, the effect is  as[m
       if ([1ma[0m) were given, but bits that are set in the umask are not affected.[m
[m
       The operator [1m+[0m causes the selected file mode bits to be added to the existing file mode bits of each file; [1m-[0m causes them to be re‐[m
       moved; and [1m=[0m causes them to be added and causes unmentioned bits to be removed except that a directory's unmentioned set user  and[m
       group ID bits are not affected.[m
[m
       The letters [1mrwxXst[0m select file mode bits for the affected users: read ([1mr[0m), write ([1mw[0m), execute (or search for directories) ([1mx[0m), ex‐[m
       ecute/search only if the file is a directory or already has execute permission for some user ([1mX[0m), set user or group ID  on  execu‐[m
       tion ([1ms[0m), restricted deletion flag or sticky bit ([1mt[0m).  Instead of one or more of these letters, you can specify exactly one of the[m
       letters [1mugo[0m: the permissions granted to the user who owns the file ([1mu[0m), the permissions granted to other users who are members  of[m
       the file's group ([1mg[0m), and the permissions granted to users that are in neither of the two preceding categories ([1mo[0m).[m
[m
       A  numeric mode is from one to four octal digits (0-7), derived by adding up the bits with values 4, 2, and 1.  Omitted digits are[m
       assumed to be leading zeros.  The first digit selects the set user ID (4) and set group ID (2) and restricted deletion  or  sticky[m
       (1)  attributes.   The  second digit selects permissions for the user who owns the file: read (4), write (2), and execute (1); the[m
       third selects permissions for other users in the file's group, with the same values; and the fourth for other  users  not  in  the[m
       file's group, with the same values.[m
[m
       [1mchmod[0m never changes the permissions of symbolic links; the [1mchmod[0m system call cannot change their permissions.  This is not a prob‐[m
       lem since the permissions of symbolic links are never used.  However, for each symbolic link listed on  the  command  line,  [1mchmod[0m[m
       changes  the permissions of the pointed-to file.  In contrast, [1mchmod[0m ignores symbolic links encountered during recursive directory[m
       traversals.[m
[m
[1mSETUID[0m [1mAND[0m [1mSETGID[0m [1mBITS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m clears the set-group-ID bit of a regular file if the file's group ID does not match the user's effective group ID or one  of[m
       the user's supplementary group IDs, unless the user has appropriate privileges.  Additional restrictions may cause the set-user-ID[m
       and set-group-ID bits of [4mMODE[24m or [4mRFILE[24m to be ignored.  This behavior depends on the policy and  functionality  of  the  underlying[m
       [1mchmod[0m system call.  When in doubt, check the underlying system behavior.[m
[m
       For  directories  [1mchmod[0m preserves set-user-ID and set-group-ID bits unless you explicitly specify otherwise.  You can set or clear[m
       the bits with symbolic modes like [1mu+s[0m and [1mg-s[0m.  To clear these bits for directories with a numeric  mode  requires  an  additional[m
       leading zero, or leading = like [1m00755[0m , or [1m=755[0m[m
[m
[1mRESTRICTED[0m [1mDELETION[0m [1mFLAG[0m [1mOR[0m [1mSTICKY[0m [1mBIT[0m[m
       The  restricted  deletion  flag or sticky bit is a single bit, whose interpretation depends on the file type.  For directories, it[m
       prevents unprivileged users from removing or renaming a file in the directory unless they own the file or the directory;  this  is[m
       called the [4mrestricted[24m [4mdeletion[24m [4mflag[24m for the directory, and is commonly found on world-writable directories like [1m/tmp[0m.  For regular[m
       files on some older systems, the bit saves the program's text image on the swap device so it will load more quickly when run; this[m
       is called the [4msticky[24m [4mbit[24m.[m
[m
[1mOPTIONS[0m[m
       Change the mode of each FILE to MODE.  With [1m--reference[0m, change the mode of each FILE to that of RFILE.[m
[m
       [1m-c[0m, [1m--changes[0m[m
              like verbose but report only when a change is made[m
[m
       [1m-f[0m, [1m--silent[0m, [1m--quiet[0m[m
              suppress most error messages[m
[m
       [1m-v[0m, [1m--verbose[0m[m
              output a diagnostic for every file processed[m
[m
       [1m--no-preserve-root[0m[m
              do not treat '/' specially (the default)[m
[m
       [1m--preserve-root[0m[m
              fail to operate recursively on '/'[m
[m
       [1m--reference[0m=[4mRFILE[24m[m
              use RFILE's mode instead of MODE values[m
[m
       [1m-R[0m, [1m--recursive[0m[m
              change files and directories recursively[m
[m
       [1m--help[0m display this help and exit[m
[m
       [1m--version[0m[m

[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 1/108 86% (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KDD[H[2J[H[H[2J[HCHMOD(1)                                                      User Commands                                                      CHMOD(1)[m
[m
[1mNAME[0m[m
       chmod - change file mode bits[m
[m
[1mSYNOPSIS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mMODE[24m[[4m,MODE[24m]... [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mOCTAL-MODE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4m--reference=RFILE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
[m
[1mDESCRIPTION[0m[m
       This manual page documents the GNU version of [1mchmod[0m.  [1mchmod[0m changes the file mode bits of each given file according to [4mmode[24m, which[m
       can be either a symbolic representation of changes to make, or an octal number representing the bit pattern for the new mode bits.[m
[m
       The format of a symbolic mode is [[1mugoa[0m...][[[1m-+=[0m][[4mperms[24m...]...], where [4mperms[24m is either zero or more letters from the set [1mrwxXst[0m, or[m
       a single letter from the set [1mugo[0m.  Multiple symbolic modes can be given, separated by commas.[m
[m
       A  combination of the letters [1mugoa[0m controls which users' access to the file will be changed: the user who owns it ([1mu[0m), other users[m
       in the file's group ([1mg[0m), other users not in the file's group ([1mo[0m), or all users ([1ma[0m).  If none of these are given, the effect is  as[m
       if ([1ma[0m) were given, but bits that are set in the umask are not affected.[m
[m
       The operator [1m+[0m causes the selected file mode bits to be added to the existing file mode bits of each file; [1m-[0m causes them to be re‐[m
       moved; and [1m=[0m causes them to be added and causes unmentioned bits to be removed except that a directory's unmentioned set user  and[m
       group ID bits are not affected.[m
[m
       The letters [1mrwxXst[0m select file mode bits for the affected users: read ([1mr[0m), write ([1mw[0m), execute (or search for directories) ([1mx[0m), ex‐[m
       ecute/search only if the file is a directory or already has execute permission for some user ([1mX[0m), set user or group ID  on  execu‐[m
       tion ([1ms[0m), restricted deletion flag or sticky bit ([1mt[0m).  Instead of one or more of these letters, you can specify exactly one of the[m
       letters [1mugo[0m: the permissions granted to the user who owns the file ([1mu[0m), the permissions granted to other users who are members  of[m
       the file's group ([1mg[0m), and the permissions granted to users that are in neither of the two preceding categories ([1mo[0m).[m
[m
       A  numeric mode is from one to four octal digits (0-7), derived by adding up the bits with values 4, 2, and 1.  Omitted digits are[m
       assumed to be leading zeros.  The first digit selects the set user ID (4) and set group ID (2) and restricted deletion  or  sticky[m
       (1)  attributes.   The  second digit selects permissions for the user who owns the file: read (4), write (2), and execute (1); the[m
       third selects permissions for other users in the file's group, with the same values; and the fourth for other  users  not  in  the[m
       file's group, with the same values.[m
[m
       [1mchmod[0m never changes the permissions of symbolic links; the [1mchmod[0m system call cannot change their permissions.  This is not a prob‐[m
       lem since the permissions of symbolic links are never used.  However, for each symbolic link listed on  the  command  line,  [1mchmod[0m[m
       changes  the permissions of the pointed-to file.  In contrast, [1mchmod[0m ignores symbolic links encountered during recursive directory[m
       traversals.[m
[m
[1mSETUID[0m [1mAND[0m [1mSETGID[0m [1mBITS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m clears the set-group-ID bit of a regular file if the file's group ID does not match the user's effective group ID or one  of[m
       the user's supplementary group IDs, unless the user has appropriate privileges.  Additional restrictions may cause the set-user-ID[m
       and set-group-ID bits of [4mMODE[24m or [4mRFILE[24m to be ignored.  This behavior depends on the policy and  functionality  of  the  underlying[m
       [1mchmod[0m system call.  When in doubt, check the underlying system behavior.[m
[m
       For  directories  [1mchmod[0m preserves set-user-ID and set-group-ID bits unless you explicitly specify otherwise.  You can set or clear[m
       the bits with symbolic modes like [1mu+s[0m and [1mg-s[0m.  To clear these bits for directories with a numeric  mode  requires  an  additional[m
       leading zero, or leading = like [1m00755[0m , or [1m=755[0m[m
[m
[1mRESTRICTED[0m [1mDELETION[0m [1mFLAG[0m [1mOR[0m [1mSTICKY[0m [1mBIT[0m[m
       The  restricted  deletion  flag or sticky bit is a single bit, whose interpretation depends on the file type.  For directories, it[m
       prevents unprivileged users from removing or renaming a file in the directory unless they own the file or the directory;  this  is[m
       called the [4mrestricted[24m [4mdeletion[24m [4mflag[24m for the directory, and is commonly found on world-writable directories like [1m/tmp[0m.  For regular[m
       files on some older systems, the bit saves the program's text image on the swap device so it will load more quickly when run; this[m
       is called the [4msticky[24m [4mbit[24m.[m
[m
[1mOPTIONS[0m[m
       Change the mode of each FILE to MODE.  With [1m--reference[0m, change the mode of each FILE to that of RFILE.[m
[m
       [1m-c[0m, [1m--changes[0m[m
              like verbose but report only when a change is made[m
[m
       [1m-f[0m, [1m--silent[0m, [1m--quiet[0m[m
              suppress most error messages[m
[m
       [1m-v[0m, [1m--verbose[0m[m
              output a diagnostic for every file processed[m
[m
       [1m--no-preserve-root[0m[m
              do not treat '/' specially (the default)[m
[m
       [1m--preserve-root[0m[m
              fail to operate recursively on '/'[m
[m
       [1m--reference[0m=[4mRFILE[24m[m
              use RFILE's mode instead of MODE values[m
[m
       [1m-R[0m, [1m--recursive[0m[m
              change files and directories recursively[m
[m
       [1m--help[0m display this help and exit[m
[m
       [1m--version[0m[m
[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 1/108 86% (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KDD[H[2J[H[H[2J[HCHMOD(1)                                                      User Commands                                                      CHMOD(1)[m

[1mRESTRICTED[0m [1mDELETION[0m [1mFLAG[0m [1mOR[0m [1mSTICKY[0m [1mBIT[0m[m

[7m Manual page chmod(1) line 1/108 86% (press h for help or q to quit)[27m[K[K [KESCESC[KOO[KDD[H[2J[H[H[2J[HCHMOD(1)                                                      User Commands                                                      CHMOD(1)[m
[m
[1mNAME[0m[m
       chmod - change file mode bits[m
[m
[1mSYNOPSIS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mMODE[24m[[4m,MODE[24m]... [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4mOCTAL-MODE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
       [1mchmod[0m [[4mOPTION[24m]... [4m--reference=RFILE[24m [4mFILE[24m...[m
[m
[1mDESCRIPTION[0m[m
       This manual page documents the GNU version of [1mchmod[0m.  [1mchmod[0m changes the file mode bits of each given file according to [4mmode[24m, which[m
       can be either a symbolic representation of changes to make, or an octal number representing the bit pattern for the new mode bits.[m
[m
       The format of a symbolic mode is [[1mugoa[0m...][[[1m-+=[0m][[4mperms[24m...]...], where [4mperms[24m is either zero or more letters from the set [1mrwxXst[0m, or[m
       a single letter from the set [1mugo[0m.  Multiple symbolic modes can be given, separated by commas.[m
[m
       A  combination of the letters [1mugoa[0m controls which users' access to the file will be changed: the user who owns it ([1mu[0m), other users[m
       in the file's group ([1mg[0m), other users not in the file's group ([1mo[0m), or all users ([1ma[0m).  If none of these are given, the effect is  as[m
       if ([1ma[0m) were given, but bits that are set in the umask are not affected.[m
[m
       The operator [1m+[0m causes the selected file mode bits to be added to the existing file mode bits of each file; [1m-[0m causes them to be re‐[m
       moved; and [1m=[0m causes them to be added and causes unmentioned bits to be removed except that a directory's unmentioned set user  and[m
       group ID bits are not affected.[m
[m
       The letters [1mrwxXst[0m select file mode bits for the affected users: read ([1mr[0m), write ([1mw[0m), execute (or search for directories) ([1mx[0m), ex‐[m
       ecute/search only if the file is a directory or already has execute permission for some user ([1mX[0m), set user or group ID  on  execu‐[m
       tion ([1ms[0m), restricted deletion flag or sticky bit ([1mt[0m).  Instead of one or more of these letters, you can specify exactly one of the[m
       letters [1mugo[0m: the permissions granted to the user who owns the file ([1mu[0m), the permissions granted to other users who are members  of[m
       the file's group ([1mg[0m), and the permissions granted to users that are in neither of the two preceding categories ([1mo[0m).[m
[m
       A  numeric mode is from one to four octal digits (0-7), derived by adding up the bits with values 4, 2, and 1.  Omitted digits are[m
       assumed to be leading zeros.  The first digit selects the set user ID (4) and set group ID (2) and restricted deletion  or  sticky[m
       (1)  attributes.   The  second digit selects permissions for the user who owns the file: read (4), write (2), and execute (1); the[m
       third selects permissions for other users in the file's group, with the same values; and the fourth for other  users  not  in  the[m
       file's group, with the same values.[m
[m
       [1mchmod[0m never changes the permissions of symbolic links; the [1mchmod[0m system call cannot change their permissions.  This is not a prob‐[m
       lem since the permissions of symbolic links are never used.  However, for each symbolic link listed on  the  command  line,  [1mchmod[0m[m
       changes  the permissions of the pointed-to file.  In contrast, [1mchmod[0m ignores symbolic links encountered during recursive directory[m
       traversals.[m
[m
[1mSETUID[0m [1mAND[0m [1mSETGID[0m [1mBITS[0m[m
       [1mchmod[0m clears the set-group-ID bit of a regular file if the file's group ID does not match the user's effective group ID or one  of[m
       the user's supplementary group IDs, unless the user has appropriate privileges.  Additional restrictions may cause the set-user-ID[m
       and set-group-ID bits of [4mMODE[24m or [4mRFILE[24m to be ignored.  This behavior depends on the policy and  functionality  of  the  underlying[m
       [1mchmod[0m system call.  When in doubt, check the underlying system behavior.[m
[m
       For  directories  [1mchmod[0m preserves set-user-ID and set-group-ID bits unless you explicitly specify otherwise.  You can set or clear[m
       the bits with symbolic modes like [1mu+s[0m and [1mg-s[0m.  To clear these bits for directories with a numeric  mode  requires  an  additional[m
       leading zero, or leading = like [1m00755[0m , or [1m=755[0m[m
[m
[1mRESTRICTED[0m [1mDELETION[0m [1mFLAG[0m [1mOR[0m [1mSTICKY[0m [1mBIT[0m[m
       The  restricted  deletion  flag or sticky bit is a single bit, whose interpretation depends on the file type.  For directories, it[m
       prevents unprivileged users from removing or renaming a file in the directory unless they own the file or the directory;  this  is[m
       called the [4mrestricted[24m [4mdeletion[24m [4mflag[24m for the directory, and is commonly found on world-writable directories like [1m/tmp[0m.  For regular[m
       files on some older systems, the bit saves the program's text image on the swap device so it will load more quickly when run; this[m
       is called the [4msticky[24m [4mbit[24m.[m
[m
[1mOPTIONS[0m[m
       Change the mode of each FILE to MODE.  With [1m--reference[0m, change the mode of each FILE to that of RFILE.[m
[m
       [1m-c[0m, [1m--changes[0m[m
              like verbose but report only when a change is made[m
[m
       [1m-f[0m, [1m--silent[0m, [1m--quiet[0m[m
              suppress most error messages[m
[m
       [1m-v[0m, [1m--verbose[0m[m
              output a diagnostic for every file processed[m
[m
       [1m--no-preserve-root[0m[m
              do not treat '/' specially (the default)[m
[m
       [1m--preserve-root[0m[m
              fail to operate recursively on '/'[m
[m
       [1m--reference[0m=[4mRFILE[24m[m
              use RFILE's mode instead of MODE values[m
[m
       [1m-R[0m, [1m--recursive[0m[m
              change files and directories recursively[m
[m
       [1m--help[0m display this help and exit[m
[m
       [1m--version[0m[m
